Yeah, uh… I’m here because things have been getting away from me. Ever since I moved into this busted-up room, I’m just not myself. I’m snapping at people over dumb stuff, and then I’m up half the night messing with leaks or trying to get the heat to work.

The guy upstairs there used to play music at like 2 a.m.—same three songs, over and over.\n\nBut, yeah, sad? I’m not sure. Maybe some days I feel low, but I don’t really know how to answer “most of the time.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge, yeah. So yeah, I guess anxious or on edge fits.\n\nDoctor: Has something really scary or traumatic happened to you since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Really scary? Avoiding? I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m scared to go places or anything.

I guess sometimes I just don’t want to deal with the room or the landlord stuff, so I put off making calls or I stay out longer than I need to. But then again, everybody avoids annoying stuff, right? Like my cousin still won’t open his mail because one time he got some ticket in there and now he acts like envelopes are out to get him.

But for me… I don’t think there’s a big thing I’m avoiding. Not that I’ve really noticed.

Lost interest? I don’t know… I still like the stuff I liked, I guess. It’s just harder to get around to it sometimes.

Like, I used to mess around watching games or fixing little things, and if something’s on I’ll still watch it. The other day I was looking at this busted shelf and thinking I could probably patch it up, then I got distracted because the guy next door was playing music too loud—same song over and over, which is wild to me.

But yeah, I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy things anymore. More like… it depends on the day, I guess.

I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. Like I’ll think about what happens if work slows down, or if something comes up I didn’t plan for, but then my head kinda jumps around.

Like yesterday I was thinking about next month, and then somehow I was thinking about this guy I used to work with who always said he had “a plan,” but his plan was just buying scratch-offs, which… I don’t know, man.

Yeah… I guess it has. Mostly with work stuff.

Like, my foreman pulled me aside and basically said if I keep slipping, I could lose my shot at moving toward crew lead. Which sucks, because I’ve been trying to get there, you know? And it’s not like I’m trying to mess up.

There’s been paperwork stuff too, and scheduling problems because of all this. I had this one form or whatever that I thought I handled, and then it turns out it wasn’t right, and then the schedule got messed up… and the office lady, she’s got this little fan on her desk that squeaks the whole time, so I’m standing there trying to talk and all I can hear is that squeak.

The guy upstairs there used to play music at like 2 a.m.—same three songs, over and over.\n\nBut, yeah, sad? I’m not sure. Maybe some days I feel low, but I don’t really know how to answer “most of the time.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge, yeah. Since I moved into that messed-up room, it’s like I’m always keyed up. I’ll be trying to sleep and then there’s a leak or the heat’s acting stupid, and I’m up messing with it instead of sleeping.\n\nThen I’m dragging myself into work late, and I start messing up things I normally know how to do. And when that happens I get snappy with people, even over dumb little stuff. Like, I’ll hear somebody say something the wrong way and I’m already fired up. So yeah, I guess anxious or on edge fits.\n\nDoctor: Has something really scary or traumatic happened to you since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Really scary? Uh… I don’t think anything like that happened. I mean, the room’s a mess and it gets me worked up, but I wouldn’t call it traumatic exactly.\n\nThere was this one night where the pipes were making this loud banging sound, and I thought something was gonna burst, but then I remembered my uncle had a place like that with old plumbing and it did the same thing. He used to smack the wall like that was gonna fix it. But, no, I don’t think anything happened like… scary-scary, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Avoiding? I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m scared to go places or anything.\n\nI guess sometimes I just don’t want to deal with the room or the landlord stuff, so I put off making calls or I stay out longer than I need to. But then again, everybody avoids annoying stuff, right? Like my cousin still won’t open his mail because one time he got some ticket in there and now he acts like envelopes are out to get him.\n\nBut for me… I don’t think there’s a big thing I’m avoiding. Not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: My weight? Uh… I don’t really know. I don’t have a scale or anything, and I don’t usually keep track like that.\n\nMy pants fit about the same, I guess. I mean, work clothes always feel weird depending on whether they just came out of the wash, you know? Like my jeans get stiff and then by lunch they’re loose again. So… I haven’t noticed some big change, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I don’t know… I wouldn’t say that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated thinking about stuff, like work and the room and money, but hopeless sounds kind of… big.\n\nSometimes I think, “How am I gonna get out of this mess?” but then I’ll start thinking about some guy I worked with who lived in this awful place for a while and then ended up moving in with his brother, and—sorry, I’m going off.\n\nI guess I haven’t really sat there thinking there’s no future or anything. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any memories or images from the past that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Memories or images… like flashbacks or something? No, I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, stuff pops into my head sometimes, but not like it’s taking over or replaying. Like I’ll remember some dumb thing from work because somebody says the same phrase as a guy I used to work with, and then I’m thinking about that guy’s truck for no reason. But that’s just normal, I think.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really had old memories coming back at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don’t know… I still like the stuff I liked, I guess. It’s just harder to get around to it sometimes.\n\nLike, I used to mess around watching games or fixing little things, and if something’s on I’ll still watch it. The other day I was looking at this busted shelf and thinking I could probably patch it up, then I got distracted because the guy next door was playing music too loud—same song over and over, which is wild to me.\n\nBut yeah, I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy things anymore. More like… it depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. Like I’ll think about what happens if work slows down, or if something comes up I didn’t plan for, but then my head kinda jumps around.\n\nLike yesterday I was thinking about next month, and then somehow I was thinking about this guy I used to work with who always said he had “a plan,” but his plan was just buying scratch-offs, which… I don’t know, man.\n\nSo, yeah, I worry some. I wouldn’t say it’s all the time, but it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Has your current situation or how you've been feeling been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. Mostly with work stuff.\n\nLike, my foreman pulled me aside and basically said if I keep slipping, I could lose my shot at moving toward crew lead. Which sucks, because I’ve been trying to get there, you know? And it’s not like I’m trying to mess up.\n\nThere’s been paperwork stuff too, and scheduling problems because of all this. I had this one form or whatever that I thought I handled, and then it turns out it wasn’t right, and then the schedule got messed up… and the office lady, she’s got this little fan on her desk that squeaks the whole time, so I’m standing there trying to talk and all I can hear is that squeak.\n\nBut yeah, daily life-wise, it’s causing problems. Work especially.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of short with people. Snapping at them over dumb stuff. Like if somebody asks me something at the wrong time, I’ll just bite their head off, and then later I’m like, why’d I do that?\n\nThat room’s been a whole thing too. Leaks, heat acting up… I’ll be up messing with it when I should be sleeping, and then I’m dragging the next day. I was late to work, and I’m messing up stuff I normally handle fine.\n\nFriends and family-wise, it’s not like some big blowout, but yeah, I haven’t been myself with people. Yeah… focusing’s been kind of rough lately. Like, I’ll be trying to do a normal thing at work, something I’ve done a hundred times, and my brain’s just not there.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, uh… I’m here because things have been getting away from me. Ever since I moved into this busted-up room, I’m just not myself. I’m snapping at people over dumb stuff, and then I’m up half the night messing with leaks or trying to get the heat to work.\n\nAnd then I drag myself into work late, and I’m screwing up things I usually handle fine. I do construction, so you know, if you’re off by a little bit it turns into a whole thing. Like one time this guy on a site—well, anyway… it’s just been piling up, and I figured I should talk to somebody before I make it worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. Like, not crying or anything like that. It’s more like… I get worn down and pissed off, and then I start thinking about how I should’ve stayed at my old place, even though that place had its own problems too. The guy upstairs there used to play music at like 2 a.m.—same three songs, over and over.\n\nBut, yeah, sad? I’m not sure. Maybe some days I feel low, but I don’t really know how to answer “most of the time.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge, yeah. Since I moved into that messed-up room, it’s like I’m always keyed up. I’ll be trying to sleep and then there’s a leak or the heat’s acting stupid, and I’m up messing with it instead of sleeping.\n\nThen I’m dragging myself into work late, and I start messing up things I normally know how to do. And when that happens I get snappy with people, even over dumb little stuff. Like, I’ll hear somebody say something the wrong way and I’m already fired up. So yeah, I guess anxious or on edge fits.\n\nDoctor: Has something really scary or traumatic happened to you since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Really scary? Uh… I don’t think anything like that happened. I mean, the room’s a mess and it gets me worked up, but I wouldn’t call it traumatic exactly.\n\nThere was this one night where the pipes were making this loud banging sound, and I thought something was gonna burst, but then I remembered my uncle had a place like that with old plumbing and it did the same thing. He used to smack the wall like that was gonna fix it. But, no, I don’t think anything happened like… scary-scary, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Avoiding? I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m scared to go places or anything.\n\nI guess sometimes I just don’t want to deal with the room or the landlord stuff, so I put off making calls or I stay out longer than I need to. But then again, everybody avoids annoying stuff, right? Like my cousin still won’t open his mail because one time he got some ticket in there and now he acts like envelopes are out to get him.\n\nBut for me… I don’t think there’s a big thing I’m avoiding. Not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: My weight? Uh… I don’t really know. I don’t have a scale or anything, and I don’t usually keep track like that.\n\nMy pants fit about the same, I guess. I mean, work clothes always feel weird depending on whether they just came out of the wash, you know? Like my jeans get stiff and then by lunch they’re loose again. So… I haven’t noticed some big change, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I don’t know… I wouldn’t say that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated thinking about stuff, like work and the room and money, but hopeless sounds kind of… big.\n\nSometimes I think, “How am I gonna get out of this mess?” but then I’ll start thinking about some guy I worked with who lived in this awful place for a while and then ended up moving in with his brother, and—sorry, I’m going off.\n\nI guess I haven’t really sat there thinking there’s no future or anything. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any memories or images from the past that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Memories or images… like flashbacks or something? No, I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, stuff pops into my head sometimes, but not like it’s taking over or replaying. Like I’ll remember some dumb thing from work because somebody says the same phrase as a guy I used to work with, and then I’m thinking about that guy’s truck for no reason. But that’s just normal, I think.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really had old memories coming back at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don’t know… I still like the stuff I liked, I guess. It’s just harder to get around to it sometimes.\n\nLike, I used to mess around watching games or fixing little things, and if something’s on I’ll still watch it. The other day I was looking at this busted shelf and thinking I could probably patch it up, then I got distracted because the guy next door was playing music too loud—same song over and over, which is wild to me.\n\nBut yeah, I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy things anymore. More like… it depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. Like I’ll think about what happens if work slows down, or if something comes up I didn’t plan for, but then my head kinda jumps around.\n\nLike yesterday I was thinking about next month, and then somehow I was thinking about this guy I used to work with who always said he had “a plan,” but his plan was just buying scratch-offs, which… I don’t know, man.\n\nSo, yeah, I worry some. I wouldn’t say it’s all the time, but it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Has your current situation or how you've been feeling been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. Mostly with work stuff.\n\nLike, my foreman pulled me aside and basically said if I keep slipping, I could lose my shot at moving toward crew lead. Which sucks, because I’ve been trying to get there, you know? And it’s not like I’m trying to mess up.\n\nThere’s been paperwork stuff too, and scheduling problems because of all this. I had this one form or whatever that I thought I handled, and then it turns out it wasn’t right, and then the schedule got messed up… and the office lady, she’s got this little fan on her desk that squeaks the whole time, so I’m standing there trying to talk and all I can hear is that squeak.\n\nBut yeah, daily life-wise, it’s causing problems. Work especially.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of short with people. Snapping at them over dumb stuff. Like if somebody asks me something at the wrong time, I’ll just bite their head off, and then later I’m like, why’d I do that?\n\nThat room’s been a whole thing too. Leaks, heat acting up… I’ll be up messing with it when I should be sleeping, and then I’m dragging the next day. I was late to work, and I’m messing up stuff I normally handle fine.\n\nFriends and family-wise, it’s not like some big blowout, but yeah, I haven’t been myself with people. And the landlord—well, not family, but still—I swear every time I try to get something fixed it turns into this whole runaround. So yeah, it’s been affecting how I deal with people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or paying attention to things lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… focusing’s been kind of rough lately. Like, I’ll be trying to do a normal thing at work, something I’ve done a hundred times, and my brain’s just not there.\n\nI mean, that’s kind of why I came in. Ever since I moved into that busted-up room, it’s like one thing after another. I’m up messing with leaks or trying to get the heat working, and then I’m dragging the next morning, showing up late, and then I’m messing up stuff I normally wouldn’t mess up.\n\nAnd then I get irritated, snap at somebody, and that doesn’t help either. So yeah… paying attention, staying on track, it’s been harder.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Guilty? Uh… I don’t know. Not really guilty, I guess.

I mean, I feel bad when I snap at people or if I’m not handling stuff the way I should, but I wouldn’t say it’s like guilt about the room or moving there. It’s more just… frustrating, you know? Like you try to get one thing settled and then there’s another thing, and then you’re thinking about rent, work, all that.

So, yeah… maybe annoyed at myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilty.

He used to smack the wall like that was gonna fix it. But, no, I don’t think anything happened like… scary-scary, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Avoiding? But then again, everybody avoids annoying stuff, right? Like my cousin still won’t open his mail because one time he got some ticket in there and now he acts like envelopes are out to get him.\n\nBut for me… I don’t think there’s a big thing I’m avoiding. Not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: My weight? Uh… I don’t really know. I don’t have a scale or anything, and I don’t usually keep track like that.\n\nMy pants fit about the same, I guess. I mean, work clothes always feel weird depending on whether they just came out of the wash, you know? Like my jeans get stiff and then by lunch they’re loose again. So… I haven’t noticed some big change, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I don’t know… I wouldn’t say that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated thinking about stuff, like work and the room and money, but hopeless sounds kind of… big.\n\nSometimes I think, “How am I gonna get out of this mess?” but then I’ll start thinking about some guy I worked with who lived in this awful place for a while and then ended up moving in with his brother, and—sorry, I’m going off.\n\nI guess I haven’t really sat there thinking there’s no future or anything. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any memories or images from the past that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Memories or images… like flashbacks or something? No, I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, stuff pops into my head sometimes, but not like it’s taking over or replaying. The other day I was looking at this busted shelf and thinking I could probably patch it up, then I got distracted because the guy next door was playing music too loud—same song over and over, which is wild to me.\n\nBut yeah, I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy things anymore. More like… it depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. Like I’ll think about what happens if work slows down, or if something comes up I didn’t plan for, but then my head kinda jumps around.\n\nLike yesterday I was thinking about next month, and then somehow I was thinking about this guy I used to work with who always said he had “a plan,” but his plan was just buying scratch-offs, which… I don’t know, man.\n\nSo, yeah, I worry some. I wouldn’t say it’s all the time, but it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Has your current situation or how you've been feeling been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. I was late to work, and I’m messing up stuff I normally handle fine.\n\nFriends and family-wise, it’s not like some big blowout, but yeah, I haven’t been myself with people. And the landlord—well, not family, but still—I swear every time I try to get something fixed it turns into this whole runaround. So yeah, it’s been affecting how I deal with people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or paying attention to things lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… focusing’s been kind of rough lately. Like, I’ll be trying to do a normal thing at work, something I’ve done a hundred times, and my brain’s just not there.\n\nI mean, that’s kind of why I came in. Ever since I moved into that busted-up room, it’s like one thing after another. I’m up messing with leaks or trying to get the heat working, and then I’m dragging the next morning, showing up late, and then I’m messing up stuff I normally wouldn’t mess up.\n\nAnd then I get irritated, snap at somebody, and that doesn’t help either. So yeah… paying attention, staying on track, it’s been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Uh… I don’t know. Not really guilty, I guess.\n\nI mean, I feel bad when I snap at people or if I’m not handling stuff the way I should, but I wouldn’t say it’s like guilt about the room or moving there. It’s more just… frustrating, you know? Like you try to get one thing settled and then there’s another thing, and then you’re thinking about rent, work, all that.\n\nSo, yeah… maybe annoyed at myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilty.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you stopped doing things you used to enjoy since moving into the new room?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / construction_laborer / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say I’ve stopped, exactly. Like… I still like the same stuff, I think. It’s just the room situation gets in my head sometimes, and then I end up dealing with whatever’s going on there instead of doing anything else.

I used to go hang out a little more, watch games, stuff like that, but it’s not like I made some decision to quit enjoying things. It’s more like the day gets eaten up, you know? Like you come home and there’s some dumb problem, and then suddenly it’s late.

So… I’m not sure. Maybe I’m doing less, but I don’t know if I’d say I stopped enjoying things.

Um… traumatic? I don’t know. I mean, depends what you mean by traumatic, I guess.

I’ve had rough stuff happen, like everybody has, you know? More like… it depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. I do construction, so you know, if you’re off by a little bit it turns into a whole thing. Like one time this guy on a site—well, anyway… it’s just been piling up, and I figured I should talk to somebody before I make it worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. Like, not crying or anything like that. It’s more like… I get worn down and pissed off, and then I start thinking about how I should’ve stayed at my old place, even though that place had its own problems too. The guy upstairs there used to play music at like 2 a.m.—same three songs, over and over.\n\nBut, yeah, sad? I’m not sure. Maybe some days I feel low, but I don’t really know how to answer “most of the time.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge, yeah. Since I moved into that messed-up room, it’s like I’m always keyed up. I’ll be trying to sleep and then there’s a leak or the heat’s acting stupid, and I’m up messing with it instead of sleeping.\n\nThen I’m dragging myself into work late, and I start messing up things I normally know how to do. And when that happens I get snappy with people, even over dumb little stuff. Like, I’ll hear somebody say something the wrong way and I’m already fired up. So yeah, I guess anxious or on edge fits.\n\nDoctor: Has something really scary or traumatic happened to you since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Really scary? Uh… I don’t think anything like that happened. I mean, the room’s a mess and it gets me worked up, but I wouldn’t call it traumatic exactly.\n\nThere was this one night where the pipes were making this loud banging sound, and I thought something was gonna burst, but then I remembered my uncle had a place like that with old plumbing and it did the same thing. He used to smack the wall like that was gonna fix it. But, no, I don’t think anything happened like… scary-scary, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Avoiding? I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m scared to go places or anything.\n\nI guess sometimes I just don’t want to deal with the room or the landlord stuff, so I put off making calls or I stay out longer than I need to. But then again, everybody avoids annoying stuff, right? Like my cousin still won’t open his mail because one time he got some ticket in there and now he acts like envelopes are out to get him.\n\nBut for me… I don’t think there’s a big thing I’m avoiding. Not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: My weight? Uh… I don’t really know. I don’t have a scale or anything, and I don’t usually keep track like that.\n\nMy pants fit about the same, I guess. I mean, work clothes always feel weird depending on whether they just came out of the wash, you know? Like my jeans get stiff and then by lunch they’re loose again. So… I haven’t noticed some big change, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I don’t know… I wouldn’t say that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated thinking about stuff, like work and the room and money, but hopeless sounds kind of… big.\n\nSometimes I think, “How am I gonna get out of this mess?” but then I’ll start thinking about some guy I worked with who lived in this awful place for a while and then ended up moving in with his brother, and—sorry, I’m going off.\n\nI guess I haven’t really sat there thinking there’s no future or anything. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any memories or images from the past that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Memories or images… like flashbacks or something? No, I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, stuff pops into my head sometimes, but not like it’s taking over or replaying. Like I’ll remember some dumb thing from work because somebody says the same phrase as a guy I used to work with, and then I’m thinking about that guy’s truck for no reason. But that’s just normal, I think.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really had old memories coming back at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don’t know… I still like the stuff I liked, I guess. It’s just harder to get around to it sometimes.\n\nLike, I used to mess around watching games or fixing little things, and if something’s on I’ll still watch it. The other day I was looking at this busted shelf and thinking I could probably patch it up, then I got distracted because the guy next door was playing music too loud—same song over and over, which is wild to me.\n\nBut yeah, I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy things anymore. More like… it depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. Like I’ll think about what happens if work slows down, or if something comes up I didn’t plan for, but then my head kinda jumps around.\n\nLike yesterday I was thinking about next month, and then somehow I was thinking about this guy I used to work with who always said he had “a plan,” but his plan was just buying scratch-offs, which… I don’t know, man.\n\nSo, yeah, I worry some. I wouldn’t say it’s all the time, but it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Has your current situation or how you've been feeling been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. Mostly with work stuff.\n\nLike, my foreman pulled me aside and basically said if I keep slipping, I could lose my shot at moving toward crew lead. Which sucks, because I’ve been trying to get there, you know? And it’s not like I’m trying to mess up.\n\nThere’s been paperwork stuff too, and scheduling problems because of all this. I had this one form or whatever that I thought I handled, and then it turns out it wasn’t right, and then the schedule got messed up… and the office lady, she’s got this little fan on her desk that squeaks the whole time, so I’m standing there trying to talk and all I can hear is that squeak.\n\nBut yeah, daily life-wise, it’s causing problems. Work especially.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of short with people. Snapping at them over dumb stuff. Like if somebody asks me something at the wrong time, I’ll just bite their head off, and then later I’m like, why’d I do that?\n\nThat room’s been a whole thing too. Leaks, heat acting up… I’ll be up messing with it when I should be sleeping, and then I’m dragging the next day. I was late to work, and I’m messing up stuff I normally handle fine.\n\nFriends and family-wise, it’s not like some big blowout, but yeah, I haven’t been myself with people. And the landlord—well, not family, but still—I swear every time I try to get something fixed it turns into this whole runaround. So yeah, it’s been affecting how I deal with people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or paying attention to things lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… focusing’s been kind of rough lately. Like, I’ll be trying to do a normal thing at work, something I’ve done a hundred times, and my brain’s just not there.\n\nI mean, that’s kind of why I came in. Ever since I moved into that busted-up room, it’s like one thing after another. I’m up messing with leaks or trying to get the heat working, and then I’m dragging the next morning, showing up late, and then I’m messing up stuff I normally wouldn’t mess up.\n\nAnd then I get irritated, snap at somebody, and that doesn’t help either. So yeah… paying attention, staying on track, it’s been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Uh… I don’t know. Not really guilty, I guess.\n\nI mean, I feel bad when I snap at people or if I’m not handling stuff the way I should, but I wouldn’t say it’s like guilt about the room or moving there. It’s more just… frustrating, you know? Like you try to get one thing settled and then there’s another thing, and then you’re thinking about rent, work, all that.\n\nSo, yeah… maybe annoyed at myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilty.\n\nDoctor: Have you stopped doing things you used to enjoy since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve stopped, exactly. Like… I still like the same stuff, I think. It’s just the room situation gets in my head sometimes, and then I end up dealing with whatever’s going on there instead of doing anything else.\n\nI used to go hang out a little more, watch games, stuff like that, but it’s not like I made some decision to quit enjoying things. It’s more like the day gets eaten up, you know? Like you come home and there’s some dumb problem, and then suddenly it’s late.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. Maybe I’m doing less, but I don’t know if I’d say I stopped enjoying things.\n\nDoctor: Has something traumatic happened to you at any point in your life, not just since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Um… traumatic? I don’t know. I mean, depends what you mean by traumatic, I guess.\n\nI’ve had rough stuff happen, like everybody has, you know? Jobs going bad, family arguments, money being tight… and construction sites, you see some messed-up stuff sometimes, like guys almost getting hurt because somebody didn’t lock something down right. One time this dude dropped a whole stack of material and everybody was yelling, and then the foreman acted like it was no big deal, which is kind of how they always do it.\n\nBut, like… if you mean some big thing that I keep thinking about or something, I’m not sure. Nothing is really coming to mind like that right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses, like the death of a loved one or the end of a relationship, since moving into the new room or in the recent past?\n\nPatient: No… not that I can think of. I mean, people come and go, you know, especially with work and rooms and places like that. One guy I used to work with quit and moved out toward his cousin’s place, but that’s not really what you’re asking.\n\nLike death or a breakup or something big like that? No, I don’t think so. Nothing like that recently. It’s more just been the whole living situation and trying to keep things steady.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Work especially.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of short with people. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Aside from the room? Um… I don’t know, not some big single thing. Work’s been kind of all over the place, but construction is always like that, you know? One week everybody’s rushing and the next week you’re standing around waiting for somebody to bring the right materials.

I mean, money’s always a thing, and dealing with people is a thing, but I wouldn’t say there was, like, a major change. Not like a new baby or somebody dying or anything. Mostly it’s just been trying to get used to that place and keep my stuff together.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.0, F32.0, F43.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, uh… I’m here because things have been getting away from me. Ever since I moved into this busted-up room, I’m just not myself. I’m snapping at people over dumb stuff, and then I’m up half the night messing with leaks or trying to get the heat to work.\n\nAnd then I drag myself into work late, and I’m screwing up things I usually handle fine. I do construction, so you know, if you’re off by a little bit it turns into a whole thing. Like one time this guy on a site—well, anyway… it’s just been piling up, and I figured I should talk to somebody before I make it worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. Like, not crying or anything like that. It’s more like… I get worn down and pissed off, and then I start thinking about how I should’ve stayed at my old place, even though that place had its own problems too. The guy upstairs there used to play music at like 2 a.m.—same three songs, over and over.\n\nBut, yeah, sad? I’m not sure. Maybe some days I feel low, but I don’t really know how to answer “most of the time.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge, yeah. Since I moved into that messed-up room, it’s like I’m always keyed up. I’ll be trying to sleep and then there’s a leak or the heat’s acting stupid, and I’m up messing with it instead of sleeping.\n\nThen I’m dragging myself into work late, and I start messing up things I normally know how to do. And when that happens I get snappy with people, even over dumb little stuff. Like, I’ll hear somebody say something the wrong way and I’m already fired up. So yeah, I guess anxious or on edge fits.\n\nDoctor: Has something really scary or traumatic happened to you since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Really scary? Uh… I don’t think anything like that happened. I mean, the room’s a mess and it gets me worked up, but I wouldn’t call it traumatic exactly.\n\nThere was this one night where the pipes were making this loud banging sound, and I thought something was gonna burst, but then I remembered my uncle had a place like that with old plumbing and it did the same thing. He used to smack the wall like that was gonna fix it. But, no, I don’t think anything happened like… scary-scary, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Avoiding? I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m scared to go places or anything.\n\nI guess sometimes I just don’t want to deal with the room or the landlord stuff, so I put off making calls or I stay out longer than I need to. But then again, everybody avoids annoying stuff, right? Like my cousin still won’t open his mail because one time he got some ticket in there and now he acts like envelopes are out to get him.\n\nBut for me… I don’t think there’s a big thing I’m avoiding. Not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: My weight? Uh… I don’t really know. I don’t have a scale or anything, and I don’t usually keep track like that.\n\nMy pants fit about the same, I guess. I mean, work clothes always feel weird depending on whether they just came out of the wash, you know? Like my jeans get stiff and then by lunch they’re loose again. So… I haven’t noticed some big change, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I don’t know… I wouldn’t say that exactly. I mean, I get frustrated thinking about stuff, like work and the room and money, but hopeless sounds kind of… big.\n\nSometimes I think, “How am I gonna get out of this mess?” but then I’ll start thinking about some guy I worked with who lived in this awful place for a while and then ended up moving in with his brother, and—sorry, I’m going off.\n\nI guess I haven’t really sat there thinking there’s no future or anything. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any memories or images from the past that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Memories or images… like flashbacks or something? No, I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, stuff pops into my head sometimes, but not like it’s taking over or replaying. Like I’ll remember some dumb thing from work because somebody says the same phrase as a guy I used to work with, and then I’m thinking about that guy’s truck for no reason. But that’s just normal, I think.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really had old memories coming back at me like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don’t know… I still like the stuff I liked, I guess. It’s just harder to get around to it sometimes.\n\nLike, I used to mess around watching games or fixing little things, and if something’s on I’ll still watch it. The other day I was looking at this busted shelf and thinking I could probably patch it up, then I got distracted because the guy next door was playing music too loud—same song over and over, which is wild to me.\n\nBut yeah, I wouldn’t say I don’t enjoy things anymore. More like… it depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. Like I’ll think about what happens if work slows down, or if something comes up I didn’t plan for, but then my head kinda jumps around.\n\nLike yesterday I was thinking about next month, and then somehow I was thinking about this guy I used to work with who always said he had “a plan,” but his plan was just buying scratch-offs, which… I don’t know, man.\n\nSo, yeah, I worry some. I wouldn’t say it’s all the time, but it comes up.\n\nDoctor: Has your current situation or how you've been feeling been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. Mostly with work stuff.\n\nLike, my foreman pulled me aside and basically said if I keep slipping, I could lose my shot at moving toward crew lead. Which sucks, because I’ve been trying to get there, you know? And it’s not like I’m trying to mess up.\n\nThere’s been paperwork stuff too, and scheduling problems because of all this. I had this one form or whatever that I thought I handled, and then it turns out it wasn’t right, and then the schedule got messed up… and the office lady, she’s got this little fan on her desk that squeaks the whole time, so I’m standing there trying to talk and all I can hear is that squeak.\n\nBut yeah, daily life-wise, it’s causing problems. Work especially.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends or family since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, since I moved into that room, I’ve been kind of short with people. Snapping at them over dumb stuff. Like if somebody asks me something at the wrong time, I’ll just bite their head off, and then later I’m like, why’d I do that?\n\nThat room’s been a whole thing too. Leaks, heat acting up… I’ll be up messing with it when I should be sleeping, and then I’m dragging the next day. I was late to work, and I’m messing up stuff I normally handle fine.\n\nFriends and family-wise, it’s not like some big blowout, but yeah, I haven’t been myself with people. And the landlord—well, not family, but still—I swear every time I try to get something fixed it turns into this whole runaround. So yeah, it’s been affecting how I deal with people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or paying attention to things lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… focusing’s been kind of rough lately. Like, I’ll be trying to do a normal thing at work, something I’ve done a hundred times, and my brain’s just not there.\n\nI mean, that’s kind of why I came in. Ever since I moved into that busted-up room, it’s like one thing after another. I’m up messing with leaks or trying to get the heat working, and then I’m dragging the next morning, showing up late, and then I’m messing up stuff I normally wouldn’t mess up.\n\nAnd then I get irritated, snap at somebody, and that doesn’t help either. So yeah… paying attention, staying on track, it’s been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Uh… I don’t know. Not really guilty, I guess.\n\nI mean, I feel bad when I snap at people or if I’m not handling stuff the way I should, but I wouldn’t say it’s like guilt about the room or moving there. It’s more just… frustrating, you know? Like you try to get one thing settled and then there’s another thing, and then you’re thinking about rent, work, all that.\n\nSo, yeah… maybe annoyed at myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilty.\n\nDoctor: Have you stopped doing things you used to enjoy since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve stopped, exactly. Like… I still like the same stuff, I think. It’s just the room situation gets in my head sometimes, and then I end up dealing with whatever’s going on there instead of doing anything else.\n\nI used to go hang out a little more, watch games, stuff like that, but it’s not like I made some decision to quit enjoying things. It’s more like the day gets eaten up, you know? Like you come home and there’s some dumb problem, and then suddenly it’s late.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. Maybe I’m doing less, but I don’t know if I’d say I stopped enjoying things.\n\nDoctor: Has something traumatic happened to you at any point in your life, not just since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Um… traumatic? I don’t know. I mean, depends what you mean by traumatic, I guess.\n\nI’ve had rough stuff happen, like everybody has, you know? Jobs going bad, family arguments, money being tight… and construction sites, you see some messed-up stuff sometimes, like guys almost getting hurt because somebody didn’t lock something down right. One time this dude dropped a whole stack of material and everybody was yelling, and then the foreman acted like it was no big deal, which is kind of how they always do it.\n\nBut, like… if you mean some big thing that I keep thinking about or something, I’m not sure. Nothing is really coming to mind like that right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses, like the death of a loved one or the end of a relationship, since moving into the new room or in the recent past?\n\nPatient: No… not that I can think of. I mean, people come and go, you know, especially with work and rooms and places like that. One guy I used to work with quit and moved out toward his cousin’s place, but that’s not really what you’re asking.\n\nLike death or a breakup or something big like that? No, I don’t think so. Nothing like that recently. It’s more just been the whole living situation and trying to keep things steady.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing anything over and over that you feel like you can't control?\n\nPatient: Doing something over and over… like what, checking stuff or washing my hands or something?\n\nI mean, I don’t think so. I’ll look around my room more than I used to, I guess, but that’s just because people mess with things, or the place is weird sometimes. It’s not like… I have to tap the door three times or anything.\n\nSometimes I’ll get stuck fixing one little thing, like moving my bag or making sure my tools are out of the way, and then I’m thinking about work stuff too, because on a site if you don’t put something where it goes somebody trips over it and then everybody’s yelling. But I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed anything like I can’t control.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a major stress or change in your life recently, aside from moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: Aside from the room? Um… I don’t know, not some big single thing. Work’s been kind of all over the place, but construction is always like that, you know? One week everybody’s rushing and the next week you’re standing around waiting for somebody to bring the right materials.\n\nI mean, money’s always a thing, and dealing with people is a thing, but I wouldn’t say there was, like, a major change. Avoiding places… I don’t know. Not really like I’m refusing to go somewhere.

I’m snapping at people over dumb stuff, and then I’m up half the night messing with leaks or trying to get the heat to work.\n\nAnd then I drag myself into work late, and I’m screwing up things I usually handle fine. I do construction, so you know, if you’re off by a little bit it turns into a whole thing. Like one time this guy on a site—well, anyway… it’s just been piling up, and I figured I should talk to somebody before I make it worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say sad most of the time. Like, not crying or anything like that. It’s more like… I get worn down and pissed off, and then I start thinking about how I should’ve stayed at my old place, even though that place had its own problems too. The guy upstairs there used to play music at like 2 a.m.—same three songs, over and over.\n\nBut, yeah, sad? I’m not sure. Maybe some days I feel low, but I don’t really know how to answer “most of the time.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge, yeah. Since I moved into that messed-up room, it’s like I’m always keyed up. I’ll be trying to sleep and then there’s a leak or the heat’s acting stupid, and I’m up messing with it instead of sleeping.\n\nThen I’m dragging myself into work late, and I start messing up things I normally know how to do. And when that happens I get snappy with people, even over dumb little stuff. Like, I’ll hear somebody say something the wrong way and I’m already fired up. So yeah, I guess anxious or on edge fits.\n\nDoctor: Has something really scary or traumatic happened to you since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Really scary? Uh… I don’t think anything like that happened. I mean, the room’s a mess and it gets me worked up, but I wouldn’t call it traumatic exactly.\n\nThere was this one night where the pipes were making this loud banging sound, and I thought something was gonna burst, but then I remembered my uncle had a place like that with old plumbing and it did the same thing. He used to smack the wall like that was gonna fix it. But, no, I don’t think anything happened like… scary-scary, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there things or situations that you've been avoiding lately?\n\nPatient: Avoiding? I don’t know… I mean, not like I’m scared to go places or anything.\n\nI guess sometimes I just don’t want to deal with the room or the landlord stuff, so I put off making calls or I stay out longer than I need to. But then again, everybody avoids annoying stuff, right? Like my cousin still won’t open his mail because one time he got some ticket in there and now he acts like envelopes are out to get him.\n\nBut for me… I don’t think there’s a big thing I’m avoiding. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any memories or images from the past that keep coming back to you?\n\nPatient: Memories or images… like flashbacks or something? No, I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, stuff pops into my head sometimes, but not like it’s taking over or replaying. More like… it depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: I mean… future stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m always worrying about it. Like I’ll think about what happens if work slows down, or if something comes up I didn’t plan for, but then my head kinda jumps around.\n\nLike yesterday I was thinking about next month, and then somehow I was thinking about this guy I used to work with who always said he had “a plan,” but his plan was just buying scratch-offs, which… I don’t know, man.\n\nSo, yeah, I worry some. Like you try to get one thing settled and then there’s another thing, and then you’re thinking about rent, work, all that.\n\nSo, yeah… maybe annoyed at myself sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilty.\n\nDoctor: Have you stopped doing things you used to enjoy since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve stopped, exactly. It’s more like the day gets eaten up, you know? Like you come home and there’s some dumb problem, and then suddenly it’s late.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. One time this dude dropped a whole stack of material and everybody was yelling, and then the foreman acted like it was no big deal, which is kind of how they always do it.\n\nBut, like… if you mean some big thing that I keep thinking about or something, I’m not sure. Nothing is really coming to mind like that right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses, like the death of a loved one or the end of a relationship, since moving into the new room or in the recent past?\n\nPatient: No… not that I can think of.
